[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23.8, F44.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because there’s this feeling I can’t really get away from, even when I’m having what I’d call a decent day. I keep feeling like certain people at my church, and some people in the walking club too, are watching me more than they should be.\n\nAnd it’s not just watching, exactly. It’s like there are little messages tucked into things—like in the church bulletin, or in comments people make while we’re walking or talking. They’ll say something ordinary, I guess, but it feels like it’s meant about me, or meant for me to notice. I know it may sound strange when I say it out loud, and I’ve tried to tell myself not to focus on it, but I can’t shake it. It stays with me, and that’s why I thought I’d better come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or looks from others are harmful, threatening, or part of some kind of plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it a plot, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church, and a few in the walking club, are paying attention to me in this very particular way. Like they’re watching how I act, or seeing if I notice things.\n\nAnd then the bulletin will have a phrase, or someone will make a comment during a walk, and it’s ordinary enough that I suppose another person might not think twice about it, but to me it feels like it’s about me. Like there’s a little message tucked in there for me to pick up on. I don’t always think it’s directly harmful, but it feels pointed, and it makes me uneasy because I can’t tell what they want from me or why they’re doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like events or comments, are specifically directed at you even when others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really the heart of it. I’ll be sitting in church and someone will read something, or I’ll look at the bulletin, and there’ll be a phrase that just seems too specific somehow. Not specific in a way I can prove, I know that, but it feels like it’s meant for me, like I’m supposed to understand there’s a message in it.\n\nAnd the same thing happens with the walking club. Someone will make a comment while we’re walking, something ordinary on the surface, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it that way?” or “Are they trying to tell me they’ve noticed something about me?” Other people don’t seem to react, so I know maybe they don’t see the connection, but I do. Or at least I feel it very strongly.\n\nEven on days when I’m doing a little better, it doesn’t fully go away. I still get this sense that certain people are watching me and slipping little messages about me into things. It makes me feel unsettled, because I can’t quite explain it, but I can’t just ignore it either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that random events, like something you see or hear, have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happens to me. That’s actually a lot of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’m not feeling as stirred up by it, I still can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people at church, and some of the people in the walking club, are watching me in a way that means something.\n\nIt’s like… I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a line or a phrase, and it will feel like it’s not just there by accident. Or someone in the walking club will say something that sounds ordinary, but the way they say it, or when they say it, makes me feel like there’s a little message in it about me. I know other people probably wouldn’t notice it, and I can understand how it might sound like I’m reading into things, but to me it feels very pointed.\n\nI don’t always know what the message is supposed to mean, exactly, and that’s part of what bothers me. It’s not always that I think something terrible is going to happen. It’s more that I feel singled out somehow, like I’m supposed to pick up on something they’re putting there for me. And then I keep turning it over in my mind afterward, wondering if they meant it, or if they were watching to see whether I noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that even people you don’t know, like strangers or those on television, are sending you messages or trying to communicate with you in some hidden way?\n\nPatient: Not so much television, no… I don’t usually feel like the television is talking to me personally, if that’s what you mean. And strangers, I’m not sure. I suppose sometimes I get uneasy around people I don’t know, but the thing that really stays with me is people I do sort of know — the people at church, and the walking club.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really, because even on my better days, when I tell myself I’m probably just overthinking it, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me. Not openly, not like they’re staring in some obvious way, but like they’re noticing things about me and then putting little messages in places where I’ll see them. Like a phrase in the church bulletin, or something someone says during a walk that sounds ordinary to everyone else, but to me it feels like it has something to do with me.\n\nI don’t know that I’d say strangers or television are communicating with me. It’s more this smaller circle of people where I keep feeling like there’s meaning hidden in what they say or what gets printed, and I’m supposed to pick up on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe something strongly that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise that it’s not true?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t know if I’d call it “believe” in a stubborn way, exactly, but there are things I feel very strongly that other people don’t seem to see at all.\n\nThe main one is what I’ve been trying to explain about church and the walking club. Even when I’m having a better day, even when I tell myself, “Maybe this is just me reading too much into it,” I still can’t really shake the feeling that some of them are watching me. Not always in a loud or obvious way, but watching enough that it feels intentional. And then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, or hear someone say something during a walk, and it feels like there’s a little message in it about me.\n\nOther people don’t seem to think that’s happening. If I try to explain it, they’ll say it’s probably just a phrase, or just a normal comment, or that nobody meant anything by it. And I can hear that, I really can. Part of me understands why they would say that. But another part of me keeps thinking, “No, but why that phrase? Why then? Why did they say it like that?” It feels too pointed to just dismiss.\n\nSo yes, even when people try to reassure me, I don’t fully believe them. I may quiet down about it because I don’t want to argue or sound strange, but inside I still feel like there’s something going on, like they’re putting these messages where I’ll notice them.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when people have shown you evidence or explained things clearly that went against your feeling that certain people were watching you or sending you messages, and even after that, you still felt just as strongly that it was true?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that has happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would say I felt just as strongly every single minute after, because sometimes when someone explains it to me I can calm down a little on the outside. But the feeling doesn’t really go away.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, someone could say, “No, that’s just a general phrase, that was for everyone,” and I can understand what they’re saying. I’m not trying to be difficult. I can sit there and nod and think, well, yes, maybe that is the reasonable explanation. But then later I’ll look at it again, or remember the timing of it, and it starts up in my head again — why that wording, why that week, why did it feel so directed at me?\n\nAnd it’s the same with the walking club. If someone says, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that,” I can hear that, but I still feel like certain people are watching me more than they let on. Not in some dramatic way, but enough that it feels like they’re noticing me and putting little messages into ordinary things they say. Even on better days, when I’m trying very hard to tell myself I’m overthinking it, I came in because I just can’t shake it. It keeps feeling like there’s something there meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: Completely certain… I don’t know if I’d use those exact words, because part of me knows how it sounds. I can sit here and say, yes, maybe there’s another explanation, maybe the bulletin was just the bulletin and the comments at the walking club were just ordinary comments.\n\nBut even when people show me or explain it clearly, I still don’t feel settled. I still come back to this feeling that some of the people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me somehow and putting little messages where I’ll notice them. It’s not like I want to believe that. I actually wish I could just let it go.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether proof makes it disappear, no… it doesn’t. It may make me quiet about it for a while, or make me try to tell myself I’m being foolish, but underneath I still feel like those things were meant for me. Even on my better days, that’s why I came in — because I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a feeling or belief about these messages or people watching you that you know others don’t share, and no matter how much they try to explain or show you otherwise, you still feel deep down that you’re right and they’re missing something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably the best way to put it. Deep down, I do feel like they’re missing something important.\n\nI know other people don’t see it the way I do. I know if I say, “That line in the church bulletin felt like it was about me,” or “That comment during the walking club felt like it was meant for me,” they’ll tell me I’m taking it too personally, or that it was just a normal thing, or that nobody is watching me. And I can understand why they say that. I’m not saying I have some perfect proof I can lay out on a table.\n\nBut the feeling is still there. Even on my better days, when I’m calmer and trying to be reasonable with myself, I can’t shake this sense that some people at church and in the walking club are noticing me in a particular way… watching me, maybe quietly, and putting little messages where I’ll pick up on them. In the bulletin, in something they say while we’re walking, little phrases that seem too specific or too well-timed.\n\nAnd when people explain it away, I may stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish or have everyone keep correcting me, but inside I still think, no, there’s something there. They’re not seeing the pattern I’m seeing. That’s part of why I came in, really, because I keep trying to talk myself out of it and I just can’t fully let it go.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: I know I keep circling back to this, but yes… even when someone gives me a perfectly sensible explanation, it doesn’t really take the feeling away.\n\nI wouldn’t say I enjoy feeling certain, because it’s upsetting, and part of me knows other people think I’m reading too much into things. But on my better days, even when I’m calm and trying to be reasonable, I still can’t shake it. That’s really why I came in. I keep feeling like some people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me in this quiet way and putting little messages about me where I’ll notice them — like in the bulletin, or in little things they say.\n\nSo if you ask whether proof makes me stop believing it was directed at me… no, not completely. I might doubt myself for a little while, or I might stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish, but underneath I still feel like there’s something there, like it was meant for me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose deep down I do feel that way, even though I know how it must sound.\n\nIt’s not that I think I’m smarter than everyone or that I want to be the only one who understands it. I actually wish someone else could either see it clearly or help me put it down. But when people tell me, “No, that wasn’t about you,” or “That look didn’t mean anything,” I can nod along, and part of me wants to believe them, but another part of me keeps thinking, no, they’re not seeing the whole thing.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Even on my better days, when I’m trying to be calm and reasonable, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me somehow. Not loudly, not openly, but in this quiet way. And then there are these little messages — in the bulletin, or in things people say while we’re walking — that feel like they’re about me. Like they’re placed there for me to notice.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do feel like I’m the one seeing what’s really going on, even when everyone else says I’m wrong. And that scares me, because I know it makes me sound foolish, but the feeling doesn’t just go away.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I know I’ve said it a few times, but that is part of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’ve slept alright and I’m not as worked up, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something going on that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nIt’s mostly with church and the walking club. I’ll notice certain people looking at me, or the way they pause before saying something, or a little line in the bulletin that seems too close to something I’ve been thinking about. And I know someone else would probably say, “That’s just a coincidence,” or “They weren’t talking about you.” I understand that, at least in my head.\n\nBut deep down it still feels like those things are connected to me somehow, like there are little messages being put there for me to notice. I don’t want to be the only one who understands it. Honestly, I’d rather not feel this way at all. But when everyone says there’s nothing there, I still have this feeling that they’re missing the pattern, and I’m the one who’s picking up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of messages or feelings of being watched in other parts of your life outside of church or the walking club, like at home, at work, or in other social settings?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most, and that’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a decent day and I’m trying to tell myself not to make too much of things, I still can’t shake that feeling.\n\nAt church it might be something in the bulletin, just a line or a phrase that feels like it’s been put there for me to notice, like it’s about something private or something I’ve been worrying over. And with the walking club, it’s more in what people say, or how they look over, or how a comment seems to land right when I’m nearby. I know that sounds like I’m connecting dots other people wouldn’t connect, but it feels directed at me.\n\nOutside of those places… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say it’s as clear to me at home or anywhere else. It’s mainly those groups of people, church and the walking club, where I feel watched in that quiet way and like there are little messages about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I’m not trying to be difficult. I can sit there and nod and think, well, yes, maybe that is the reasonable explanation. But then later I’ll look at it again, or remember the timing of it, and it starts up in my head again — why that wording, why that week, why did it feel so directed at me?\n\nAnd it’s the same with the walking club. If someone says, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that,” I can hear that, but I still feel like certain people are watching me more than they let on. Not in some dramatic way, but enough that it feels like they’re noticing me and putting little messages into ordinary things they say. Even on better days, when I’m trying very hard to tell myself I’m overthinking it, I came in because I just can’t shake it. It keeps feeling like there’s something there meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: Completely certain… I don’t know if I’d use those exact words, because part of me knows how it sounds. I can sit here and say, yes, maybe there’s another explanation, maybe the bulletin was just the bulletin and the comments at the walking club were just ordinary comments.\n\nBut even when people show me or explain it clearly, I still don’t feel settled. I still come back to this feeling that some of the people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me somehow and putting little messages where I’ll notice them. It’s not like I want to believe that. I actually wish I could just let it go.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether proof makes it disappear, no… it doesn’t. It may make me quiet about it for a while, or make me try to tell myself I’m being foolish, but underneath I still feel like those things were meant for me. Even on my better days, that’s why I came in — because I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a feeling or belief about these messages or people watching you that you know others don’t share, and no matter how much they try to explain or show you otherwise, you still feel deep down that you’re right and they’re missing something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably the best way to put it. Deep down, I do feel like they’re missing something important.\n\nI know other people don’t see it the way I do. I know if I say, “That line in the church bulletin felt like it was about me,” or “That comment during the walking club felt like it was meant for me,” they’ll tell me I’m taking it too personally, or that it was just a normal thing, or that nobody is watching me. And I can understand why they say that. I’m not saying I have some perfect proof I can lay out on a table.\n\nBut the feeling is still there. Even on my better days, when I’m calmer and trying to be reasonable with myself, I can’t shake this sense that some people at church and in the walking club are noticing me in a particular way… watching me, maybe quietly, and putting little messages where I’ll pick up on them. In the bulletin, in something they say while we’re walking, little phrases that seem too specific or too well-timed.\n\nAnd when people explain it away, I may stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish or have everyone keep correcting me, but inside I still think, no, there’s something there. They’re not seeing the pattern I’m seeing. That’s part of why I came in, really, because I keep trying to talk myself out of it and I just can’t fully let it go.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: I know I keep circling back to this, but yes… even when someone gives me a perfectly sensible explanation, it doesn’t really take the feeling away.\n\nI wouldn’t say I enjoy feeling certain, because it’s upsetting, and part of me knows other people think I’m reading too much into things. But on my better days, even when I’m calm and trying to be reasonable, I still can’t shake it. That’s really why I came in. I keep feeling like some people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me in this quiet way and putting little messages about me where I’ll notice them — like in the bulletin, or in little things they say.\n\nSo if you ask whether proof makes me stop believing it was directed at me… no, not completely. I might doubt myself for a little while, or I might stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish, but underneath I still feel like there’s something there, like it was meant for me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose deep down I do feel that way, even though I know how it must sound.\n\nIt’s not that I think I’m smarter than everyone or that I want to be the only one who understands it. I actually wish someone else could either see it clearly or help me put it down. But when people tell me, “No, that wasn’t about you,” or “That look didn’t mean anything,” I can nod along, and part of me wants to believe them, but another part of me keeps thinking, no, they’re not seeing the whole thing.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Even on my better days, when I’m trying to be calm and reasonable, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me somehow. Not loudly, not openly, but in this quiet way. And then there are these little messages — in the bulletin, or in things people say while we’re walking — that feel like they’re about me. Like they’re placed there for me to notice.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do feel like I’m the one seeing what’s really going on, even when everyone else says I’m wrong. And that scares me, because I know it makes me sound foolish, but the feeling doesn’t just go away.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I know I’ve said it a few times, but that is part of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’ve slept alright and I’m not as worked up, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something going on that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nIt’s mostly with church and the walking club. I’ll notice certain people looking at me, or the way they pause before saying something, or a little line in the bulletin that seems too close to something I’ve been thinking about. And I know someone else would probably say, “That’s just a coincidence,” or “They weren’t talking about you.” I understand that, at least in my head.\n\nBut deep down it still feels like those things are connected to me somehow, like there are little messages being put there for me to notice. I don’t want to be the only one who understands it. Honestly, I’d rather not feel this way at all. But when everyone says there’s nothing there, I still have this feeling that they’re missing the pattern, and I’m the one who’s picking up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of messages or feelings of being watched in other parts of your life outside of church or the walking club, like at home, at work, or in other social settings?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most, and that’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a decent day and I’m trying to tell myself not to make too much of things, I still can’t shake that feeling.\n\nAt church it might be something in the bulletin, just a line or a phrase that feels like it’s been put there for me to notice, like it’s about something private or something I’ve been worrying over. And with the walking club, it’s more in what people say, or how they look over, or how a comment seems to land right when I’m nearby. I know that sounds like I’m connecting dots other people wouldn’t connect, but it feels directed at me.\n\nOutside of those places… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say it’s as clear to me at home or anywhere else. It’s mainly those groups of people, church and the walking club, where I feel watched in that quiet way and like there are little messages about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if there are other beliefs exactly… not in the way people might mean that. The main thing, the thing I keep coming back to, is what I’ve already been trying to explain about church and the walking club.

It’s just that even on my better days — and I do have days where I’m calmer and I tell myself, “Maybe you’re making too much of this” — I still can’t shake it. I’ll be at church and something in the bulletin will stand out, just a phrase or a line, and it feels like it’s been put there for me. Not in a big obvious way, but in a small way, like someone is trying to say something without saying it directly. And then at the walking club, it can be a look someone gives me, or a comment that seems to come right when I’m nearby, and it feels like they’re watching me or talking around me.

Other people don’t seem to see it that way. They’ll say, “No, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They weren’t looking at you,” and I do hear them. I’m not trying to argue with everyone. But inside, it still feels like there’s something there, like little messages about me that I’m supposed to notice.

So I suppose yes, if that counts as an unusual belief, then yes. That’s the one I can’t seem to put down.

I know it may sound strange when I say it out loud, and I’ve tried to tell myself not to focus on it, but I can’t shake it. It stays with me, and that’s why I thought I’d better come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or looks from others are harmful, threatening, or part of some kind of plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it a plot, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church, and a few in the walking club, are paying attention to me in this very particular way. Like they’re watching how I act, or seeing if I notice things.\n\nAnd then the bulletin will have a phrase, or someone will make a comment during a walk, and it’s ordinary enough that I suppose another person might not think twice about it, but to me it feels like it’s about me. Like there’s a little message tucked in there for me to pick up on. I don’t always think it’s directly harmful, but it feels pointed, and it makes me uneasy because I can’t tell what they want from me or why they’re doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like events or comments, are specifically directed at you even when others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really the heart of it. I’ll be sitting in church and someone will read something, or I’ll look at the bulletin, and there’ll be a phrase that just seems too specific somehow. Not specific in a way I can prove, I know that, but it feels like it’s meant for me, like I’m supposed to understand there’s a message in it.\n\nAnd the same thing happens with the walking club. Someone will make a comment while we’re walking, something ordinary on the surface, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it that way?” or “Are they trying to tell me they’ve noticed something about me?” Other people don’t seem to react, so I know maybe they don’t see the connection, but I do. Or at least I feel it very strongly.\n\nEven on days when I’m doing a little better, it doesn’t fully go away. I still get this sense that certain people are watching me and slipping little messages about me into things. It makes me feel unsettled, because I can’t quite explain it, but I can’t just ignore it either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that random events, like something you see or hear, have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happens to me. That’s actually a lot of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’m not feeling as stirred up by it, I still can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people at church, and some of the people in the walking club, are watching me in a way that means something.\n\nIt’s like… I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a line or a phrase, and it will feel like it’s not just there by accident. Or someone in the walking club will say something that sounds ordinary, but the way they say it, or when they say it, makes me feel like there’s a little message in it about me. I know other people probably wouldn’t notice it, and I can understand how it might sound like I’m reading into things, but to me it feels very pointed.\n\nI don’t always know what the message is supposed to mean, exactly, and that’s part of what bothers me. It’s not always that I think something terrible is going to happen. It’s more that I feel singled out somehow, like I’m supposed to pick up on something they’re putting there for me. And then I keep turning it over in my mind afterward, wondering if they meant it, or if they were watching to see whether I noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that even people you don’t know, like strangers or those on television, are sending you messages or trying to communicate with you in some hidden way?\n\nPatient: Not so much television, no… I don’t usually feel like the television is talking to me personally, if that’s what you mean. And strangers, I’m not sure. I suppose sometimes I get uneasy around people I don’t know, but the thing that really stays with me is people I do sort of know — the people at church, and the walking club.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really, because even on my better days, when I tell myself I’m probably just overthinking it, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me. Not openly, not like they’re staring in some obvious way, but like they’re noticing things about me and then putting little messages in places where I’ll see them. Like a phrase in the church bulletin, or something someone says during a walk that sounds ordinary to everyone else, but to me it feels like it has something to do with me.\n\nI don’t know that I’d say strangers or television are communicating with me. It’s more this smaller circle of people where I keep feeling like there’s meaning hidden in what they say or what gets printed, and I’m supposed to pick up on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe something strongly that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise that it’s not true?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t know if I’d call it “believe” in a stubborn way, exactly, but there are things I feel very strongly that other people don’t seem to see at all.\n\nThe main one is what I’ve been trying to explain about church and the walking club. Even when I’m having a better day, even when I tell myself, “Maybe this is just me reading too much into it,” I still can’t really shake the feeling that some of them are watching me. Not always in a loud or obvious way, but watching enough that it feels intentional. And then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, or hear someone say something during a walk, and it feels like there’s a little message in it about me.\n\nOther people don’t seem to think that’s happening. If I try to explain it, they’ll say it’s probably just a phrase, or just a normal comment, or that nobody meant anything by it. And I can hear that, I really can. Part of me understands why they would say that. But another part of me keeps thinking, “No, but why that phrase? Why then? Why did they say it like that?” It feels too pointed to just dismiss.\n\nSo yes, even when people try to reassure me, I don’t fully believe them. I may quiet down about it because I don’t want to argue or sound strange, but inside I still feel like there’s something going on, like they’re putting these messages where I’ll notice them.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when people have shown you evidence or explained things clearly that went against your feeling that certain people were watching you or sending you messages, and even after that, you still felt just as strongly that it was true?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that has happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would say I felt just as strongly every single minute after, because sometimes when someone explains it to me I can calm down a little on the outside. But the feeling doesn’t really go away.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, someone could say, “No, that’s just a general phrase, that was for everyone,” and I can understand what they’re saying. I’m not trying to be difficult. I can sit there and nod and think, well, yes, maybe that is the reasonable explanation. But then later I’ll look at it again, or remember the timing of it, and it starts up in my head again — why that wording, why that week, why did it feel so directed at me?\n\nAnd it’s the same with the walking club. If someone says, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that,” I can hear that, but I still feel like certain people are watching me more than they let on. Not in some dramatic way, but enough that it feels like they’re noticing me and putting little messages into ordinary things they say. Even on better days, when I’m trying very hard to tell myself I’m overthinking it, I came in because I just can’t shake it. It keeps feeling like there’s something there meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: Completely certain… I don’t know if I’d use those exact words, because part of me knows how it sounds. I can sit here and say, yes, maybe there’s another explanation, maybe the bulletin was just the bulletin and the comments at the walking club were just ordinary comments.\n\nBut even when people show me or explain it clearly, I still don’t feel settled. I still come back to this feeling that some of the people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me somehow and putting little messages where I’ll notice them. It’s not like I want to believe that. I actually wish I could just let it go.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether proof makes it disappear, no… it doesn’t. It may make me quiet about it for a while, or make me try to tell myself I’m being foolish, but underneath I still feel like those things were meant for me. Even on my better days, that’s why I came in — because I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a feeling or belief about these messages or people watching you that you know others don’t share, and no matter how much they try to explain or show you otherwise, you still feel deep down that you’re right and they’re missing something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably the best way to put it. Deep down, I do feel like they’re missing something important.\n\nI know other people don’t see it the way I do. I know if I say, “That line in the church bulletin felt like it was about me,” or “That comment during the walking club felt like it was meant for me,” they’ll tell me I’m taking it too personally, or that it was just a normal thing, or that nobody is watching me. And I can understand why they say that. I’m not saying I have some perfect proof I can lay out on a table.\n\nBut the feeling is still there. Even on my better days, when I’m calmer and trying to be reasonable with myself, I can’t shake this sense that some people at church and in the walking club are noticing me in a particular way… watching me, maybe quietly, and putting little messages where I’ll pick up on them. In the bulletin, in something they say while we’re walking, little phrases that seem too specific or too well-timed.\n\nAnd when people explain it away, I may stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish or have everyone keep correcting me, but inside I still think, no, there’s something there. They’re not seeing the pattern I’m seeing. That’s part of why I came in, really, because I keep trying to talk myself out of it and I just can’t fully let it go.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: I know I keep circling back to this, but yes… even when someone gives me a perfectly sensible explanation, it doesn’t really take the feeling away.\n\nI wouldn’t say I enjoy feeling certain, because it’s upsetting, and part of me knows other people think I’m reading too much into things. But on my better days, even when I’m calm and trying to be reasonable, I still can’t shake it. That’s really why I came in. I keep feeling like some people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me in this quiet way and putting little messages about me where I’ll notice them — like in the bulletin, or in little things they say.\n\nSo if you ask whether proof makes me stop believing it was directed at me… no, not completely. I might doubt myself for a little while, or I might stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish, but underneath I still feel like there’s something there, like it was meant for me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose deep down I do feel that way, even though I know how it must sound.\n\nIt’s not that I think I’m smarter than everyone or that I want to be the only one who understands it. I actually wish someone else could either see it clearly or help me put it down. But when people tell me, “No, that wasn’t about you,” or “That look didn’t mean anything,” I can nod along, and part of me wants to believe them, but another part of me keeps thinking, no, they’re not seeing the whole thing.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Even on my better days, when I’m trying to be calm and reasonable, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me somehow. Not loudly, not openly, but in this quiet way. And then there are these little messages — in the bulletin, or in things people say while we’re walking — that feel like they’re about me. Like they’re placed there for me to notice.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do feel like I’m the one seeing what’s really going on, even when everyone else says I’m wrong. And that scares me, because I know it makes me sound foolish, but the feeling doesn’t just go away.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I know I’ve said it a few times, but that is part of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’ve slept alright and I’m not as worked up, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something going on that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nIt’s mostly with church and the walking club. I’ll notice certain people looking at me, or the way they pause before saying something, or a little line in the bulletin that seems too close to something I’ve been thinking about. And I know someone else would probably say, “That’s just a coincidence,” or “They weren’t talking about you.” I understand that, at least in my head.\n\nBut deep down it still feels like those things are connected to me somehow, like there are little messages being put there for me to notice. I don’t want to be the only one who understands it. Honestly, I’d rather not feel this way at all. But when everyone says there’s nothing there, I still have this feeling that they’re missing the pattern, and I’m the one who’s picking up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of messages or feelings of being watched in other parts of your life outside of church or the walking club, like at home, at work, or in other social settings?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most, and that’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a decent day and I’m trying to tell myself not to make too much of things, I still can’t shake that feeling.\n\nAt church it might be something in the bulletin, just a line or a phrase that feels like it’s been put there for me to notice, like it’s about something private or something I’ve been worrying over. And with the walking club, it’s more in what people say, or how they look over, or how a comment seems to land right when I’m nearby. I know that sounds like I’m connecting dots other people wouldn’t connect, but it feels directed at me.\n\nOutside of those places… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say it’s as clear to me at home or anywhere else. It’s mainly those groups of people, church and the walking club, where I feel watched in that quiet way and like there are little messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or thoughts that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there are other beliefs exactly… not in the way people might mean that. The main thing, the thing I keep coming back to, is what I’ve already been trying to explain about church and the walking club.\n\nIt’s just that even on my better days — and I do have days where I’m calmer and I tell myself, “Maybe you’re making too much of this” — I still can’t shake it. I’ll be at church and something in the bulletin will stand out, just a phrase or a line, and it feels like it’s been put there for me. Not in a big obvious way, but in a small way, like someone is trying to say something without saying it directly. And then at the walking club, it can be a look someone gives me, or a comment that seems to come right when I’m nearby, and it feels like they’re watching me or talking around me.\n\nOther people don’t seem to see it that way. They’ll say, “No, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They weren’t looking at you,” and I do hear them. I’m not trying to argue with everyone. But inside, it still feels like there’s something there, like little messages about me that I’m supposed to notice.\n\nSo I suppose yes, if that counts as an unusual belief, then yes. That’s the one I can’t seem to put down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But when people tell me, “No, that wasn’t about you,” or “That look didn’t mean anything,” I can nod along, and part of me wants to believe them, but another part of me keeps thinking, no, they’re not seeing the whole thing.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Even on my better days, when I’m trying to be calm and reasonable, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me somehow. Not loudly, not openly, but in this quiet way. And then there are these little messages — in the bulletin, or in things people say while we’re walking — that feel like they’re about me. Like they’re placed there for me to notice.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do feel like I’m the one seeing what’s really going on, even when everyone else says I’m wrong. And that scares me, because I know it makes me sound foolish, but the feeling doesn’t just go away.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I know I’ve said it a few times, but that is part of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’ve slept alright and I’m not as worked up, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something going on that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nIt’s mostly with church and the walking club. I’ll notice certain people looking at me, or the way they pause before saying something, or a little line in the bulletin that seems too close to something I’ve been thinking about. And I know someone else would probably say, “That’s just a coincidence,” or “They weren’t talking about you.” I understand that, at least in my head.\n\nBut deep down it still feels like those things are connected to me somehow, like there are little messages being put there for me to notice. I don’t want to be the only one who understands it. Honestly, I’d rather not feel this way at all. But when everyone says there’s nothing there, I still have this feeling that they’re missing the pattern, and I’m the one who’s picking up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of messages or feelings of being watched in other parts of your life outside of church or the walking club, like at home, at work, or in other social settings?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most, and that’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a decent day and I’m trying to tell myself not to make too much of things, I still can’t shake that feeling.\n\nAt church it might be something in the bulletin, just a line or a phrase that feels like it’s been put there for me to notice, like it’s about something private or something I’ve been worrying over. I know that sounds like I’m connecting dots other people wouldn’t connect, but it feels directed at me.\n\nOutside of those places… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say it’s as clear to me at home or anywhere else. It’s mainly those groups of people, church and the walking club, where I feel watched in that quiet way and like there are little messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or thoughts that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there are other beliefs exactly… not in the way people might mean that. The main thing, the thing I keep coming back to, is what I’ve already been trying to explain about church and the walking club.\n\nIt’s just that even on my better days — and I do have days where I’m calmer and I tell myself, “Maybe you’re making too much of this” — I still can’t shake it. I’ll be at church and something in the bulletin will stand out, just a phrase or a line, and it feels like it’s been put there for me. Not in a big obvious way, but in a small way, like someone is trying to say something without saying it directly. I don’t know if I’d call it a whole organized plan or a conspiracy exactly. That sounds… bigger than how I experience it, I guess.

It’s more that I came in because I can’t shake this feeling, even on my better days, that certain people at church and in the walking club are watching me in this quiet way. And then there are these little things — a line in the church bulletin, or something someone says during the walk, or the way a comment seems to come at just the right moment — and it feels like it’s about me, like it’s been placed there for me to pick up on.

I’m not saying I have proof that they all sat down and planned it out. I don’t know that. But it does feel connected somehow, like more than just random things happening. And that’s what bothers me, because I’ll try to talk myself out of it, and I’ll think, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” but then something else happens and I’m right back to feeling like there are little messages about me that I’m supposed to notice.

I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because there’s this feeling I can’t really get away from, even when I’m having what I’d call a decent day. It stays with me, and that’s why I thought I’d better come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or looks from others are harmful, threatening, or part of some kind of plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it a plot, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church, and a few in the walking club, are paying attention to me in this very particular way. Like they’re watching how I act, or seeing if I notice things.\n\nAnd then the bulletin will have a phrase, or someone will make a comment during a walk, and it’s ordinary enough that I suppose another person might not think twice about it, but to me it feels like it’s about me. Like there’s a little message tucked in there for me to pick up on. I don’t always think it’s directly harmful, but it feels pointed, and it makes me uneasy because I can’t tell what they want from me or why they’re doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like events or comments, are specifically directed at you even when others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really the heart of it. I’ll be sitting in church and someone will read something, or I’ll look at the bulletin, and there’ll be a phrase that just seems too specific somehow. Not specific in a way I can prove, I know that, but it feels like it’s meant for me, like I’m supposed to understand there’s a message in it.\n\nAnd the same thing happens with the walking club. Someone will make a comment while we’re walking, something ordinary on the surface, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it that way?” or “Are they trying to tell me they’ve noticed something about me?” Other people don’t seem to react, so I know maybe they don’t see the connection, but I do. Or at least I feel it very strongly.\n\nEven on days when I’m doing a little better, it doesn’t fully go away. I still get this sense that certain people are watching me and slipping little messages about me into things. It makes me feel unsettled, because I can’t quite explain it, but I can’t just ignore it either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that random events, like something you see or hear, have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happens to me. That’s actually a lot of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’m not feeling as stirred up by it, I still can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people at church, and some of the people in the walking club, are watching me in a way that means something.\n\nIt’s like… I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a line or a phrase, and it will feel like it’s not just there by accident. Or someone in the walking club will say something that sounds ordinary, but the way they say it, or when they say it, makes me feel like there’s a little message in it about me. I know other people probably wouldn’t notice it, and I can understand how it might sound like I’m reading into things, but to me it feels very pointed.\n\nI don’t always know what the message is supposed to mean, exactly, and that’s part of what bothers me. It’s not always that I think something terrible is going to happen. It’s more that I feel singled out somehow, like I’m supposed to pick up on something they’re putting there for me. And then I keep turning it over in my mind afterward, wondering if they meant it, or if they were watching to see whether I noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that even people you don’t know, like strangers or those on television, are sending you messages or trying to communicate with you in some hidden way?\n\nPatient: Not so much television, no… I don’t usually feel like the television is talking to me personally, if that’s what you mean. And strangers, I’m not sure. I suppose sometimes I get uneasy around people I don’t know, but the thing that really stays with me is people I do sort of know — the people at church, and the walking club.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really, because even on my better days, when I tell myself I’m probably just overthinking it, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me. Not openly, not like they’re staring in some obvious way, but like they’re noticing things about me and then putting little messages in places where I’ll see them. Like a phrase in the church bulletin, or something someone says during a walk that sounds ordinary to everyone else, but to me it feels like it has something to do with me.\n\nI don’t know that I’d say strangers or television are communicating with me. It’s more this smaller circle of people where I keep feeling like there’s meaning hidden in what they say or what gets printed, and I’m supposed to pick up on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe something strongly that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise that it’s not true?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t know if I’d call it “believe” in a stubborn way, exactly, but there are things I feel very strongly that other people don’t seem to see at all.\n\nThe main one is what I’ve been trying to explain about church and the walking club. Even when I’m having a better day, even when I tell myself, “Maybe this is just me reading too much into it,” I still can’t really shake the feeling that some of them are watching me. Not always in a loud or obvious way, but watching enough that it feels intentional. And then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, or hear someone say something during a walk, and it feels like there’s a little message in it about me.\n\nOther people don’t seem to think that’s happening. If I try to explain it, they’ll say it’s probably just a phrase, or just a normal comment, or that nobody meant anything by it. And I can hear that, I really can. Part of me understands why they would say that. But another part of me keeps thinking, “No, but why that phrase? Why then? Why did they say it like that?” It feels too pointed to just dismiss.\n\nSo yes, even when people try to reassure me, I don’t fully believe them. I may quiet down about it because I don’t want to argue or sound strange, but inside I still feel like there’s something going on, like they’re putting these messages where I’ll notice them.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when people have shown you evidence or explained things clearly that went against your feeling that certain people were watching you or sending you messages, and even after that, you still felt just as strongly that it was true?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that has happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would say I felt just as strongly every single minute after, because sometimes when someone explains it to me I can calm down a little on the outside. But the feeling doesn’t really go away.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, someone could say, “No, that’s just a general phrase, that was for everyone,” and I can understand what they’re saying. I’m not trying to be difficult. I can sit there and nod and think, well, yes, maybe that is the reasonable explanation. But then later I’ll look at it again, or remember the timing of it, and it starts up in my head again — why that wording, why that week, why did it feel so directed at me?\n\nAnd it’s the same with the walking club. If someone says, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that,” I can hear that, but I still feel like certain people are watching me more than they let on. Not in some dramatic way, but enough that it feels like they’re noticing me and putting little messages into ordinary things they say. Even on better days, when I’m trying very hard to tell myself I’m overthinking it, I came in because I just can’t shake it. It keeps feeling like there’s something there meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: Completely certain… I don’t know if I’d use those exact words, because part of me knows how it sounds. I can sit here and say, yes, maybe there’s another explanation, maybe the bulletin was just the bulletin and the comments at the walking club were just ordinary comments.\n\nBut even when people show me or explain it clearly, I still don’t feel settled. I still come back to this feeling that some of the people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me somehow and putting little messages where I’ll notice them. It’s not like I want to believe that. I actually wish I could just let it go.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether proof makes it disappear, no… it doesn’t. It may make me quiet about it for a while, or make me try to tell myself I’m being foolish, but underneath I still feel like those things were meant for me. Even on my better days, that’s why I came in — because I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a feeling or belief about these messages or people watching you that you know others don’t share, and no matter how much they try to explain or show you otherwise, you still feel deep down that you’re right and they’re missing something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably the best way to put it. Deep down, I do feel like they’re missing something important.\n\nI know other people don’t see it the way I do. I know if I say, “That line in the church bulletin felt like it was about me,” or “That comment during the walking club felt like it was meant for me,” they’ll tell me I’m taking it too personally, or that it was just a normal thing, or that nobody is watching me. And I can understand why they say that. I’m not saying I have some perfect proof I can lay out on a table.\n\nBut the feeling is still there. Even on my better days, when I’m calmer and trying to be reasonable with myself, I can’t shake this sense that some people at church and in the walking club are noticing me in a particular way… watching me, maybe quietly, and putting little messages where I’ll pick up on them. In the bulletin, in something they say while we’re walking, little phrases that seem too specific or too well-timed.\n\nAnd when people explain it away, I may stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish or have everyone keep correcting me, but inside I still think, no, there’s something there. They’re not seeing the pattern I’m seeing. That’s part of why I came in, really, because I keep trying to talk myself out of it and I just can’t fully let it go.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that the things you were noticing weren’t meant for you or weren’t about you, do you still feel completely certain that they were, in fact, directed at you?\n\nPatient: I know I keep circling back to this, but yes… even when someone gives me a perfectly sensible explanation, it doesn’t really take the feeling away.\n\nI wouldn’t say I enjoy feeling certain, because it’s upsetting, and part of me knows other people think I’m reading too much into things. But on my better days, even when I’m calm and trying to be reasonable, I still can’t shake it. That’s really why I came in. I keep feeling like some people at church, and some people in the walking club, are watching me in this quiet way and putting little messages about me where I’ll notice them — like in the bulletin, or in little things they say.\n\nSo if you ask whether proof makes me stop believing it was directed at me… no, not completely. I might doubt myself for a little while, or I might stop talking about it because I don’t want to sound foolish, but underneath I still feel like there’s something there, like it was meant for me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose deep down I do feel that way, even though I know how it must sound.\n\nIt’s not that I think I’m smarter than everyone or that I want to be the only one who understands it. I actually wish someone else could either see it clearly or help me put it down. But when people tell me, “No, that wasn’t about you,” or “That look didn’t mean anything,” I can nod along, and part of me wants to believe them, but another part of me keeps thinking, no, they’re not seeing the whole thing.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Even on my better days, when I’m trying to be calm and reasonable, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me somehow. Not loudly, not openly, but in this quiet way. And then there are these little messages — in the bulletin, or in things people say while we’re walking — that feel like they’re about me. Like they’re placed there for me to notice.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do feel like I’m the one seeing what’s really going on, even when everyone else says I’m wrong. And that scares me, because I know it makes me sound foolish, but the feeling doesn’t just go away.\n\nDoctor: Even when people have shown you clear proof that these messages or looks weren’t about you, do you still feel deep down that you’re the only one who truly understands what’s really going on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I know I’ve said it a few times, but that is part of why I came in. Even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I’ve slept alright and I’m not as worked up, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something going on that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nIt’s mostly with church and the walking club. I’ll notice certain people looking at me, or the way they pause before saying something, or a little line in the bulletin that seems too close to something I’ve been thinking about. And I know someone else would probably say, “That’s just a coincidence,” or “They weren’t talking about you.” I understand that, at least in my head.\n\nBut deep down it still feels like those things are connected to me somehow, like there are little messages being put there for me to notice. I don’t want to be the only one who understands it. Honestly, I’d rather not feel this way at all. But when everyone says there’s nothing there, I still have this feeling that they’re missing the pattern, and I’m the one who’s picking up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of messages or feelings of being watched in other parts of your life outside of church or the walking club, like at home, at work, or in other social settings?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most, and that’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a decent day and I’m trying to tell myself not to make too much of things, I still can’t shake that feeling.\n\nAt church it might be something in the bulletin, just a line or a phrase that feels like it’s been put there for me to notice, like it’s about something private or something I’ve been worrying over. And with the walking club, it’s more in what people say, or how they look over, or how a comment seems to land right when I’m nearby. I know that sounds like I’m connecting dots other people wouldn’t connect, but it feels directed at me.\n\nOutside of those places… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say it’s as clear to me at home or anywhere else. It’s mainly those groups of people, church and the walking club, where I feel watched in that quiet way and like there are little messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or thoughts that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there are other beliefs exactly… not in the way people might mean that. The main thing, the thing I keep coming back to, is what I’ve already been trying to explain about church and the walking club.\n\nIt’s just that even on my better days — and I do have days where I’m calmer and I tell myself, “Maybe you’re making too much of this” — I still can’t shake it. I’ll be at church and something in the bulletin will stand out, just a phrase or a line, and it feels like it’s been put there for me. Not in a big obvious way, but in a small way, like someone is trying to say something without saying it directly. And then at the walking club, it can be a look someone gives me, or a comment that seems to come right when I’m nearby, and it feels like they’re watching me or talking around me.\n\nOther people don’t seem to see it that way. They’ll say, “No, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They weren’t looking at you,” and I do hear them. I’m not trying to argue with everyone. But inside, it still feels like there’s something there, like little messages about me that I’m supposed to notice.\n\nSo I suppose yes, if that counts as an unusual belief, then yes. That’s the one I can’t seem to put down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually involved in some kind of organized plan or conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole organized plan or a conspiracy exactly. That sounds… bigger than how I experience it, I guess.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because I can’t shake this feeling, even on my better days, that certain people at church and in the walking club are watching me in this quiet way. Like they notice me more than they let on. And then there are these little things — a line in the church bulletin, or something someone says during the walk, or the way a comment seems to come at just the right moment — and it feels like it’s about me, like it’s been placed there for me to pick up on.\n\nI’m not saying I have proof that they all sat down and planned it out. I don’t know that. But it does feel connected somehow, like more than just random things happening. And that’s what bothers me, because I’ll try to talk myself out of it, and I’ll think, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” but then something else happens and I’m right back to feeling like there are little messages about me that I’m supposed to notice.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s where I notice it the most, and that’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a decent day and I’m trying to tell myself not to make too much of things, I still can’t shake that feeling.\n\nAt church it might be something in the bulletin, just a line or a phrase that feels like it’s been put there for me to notice, like it’s about something private or something I’ve been worrying over. And with the walking club, it’s more in what people say, or how they look over, or how a comment seems to land right when I’m nearby. I know that sounds like I’m connecting dots other people wouldn’t connect, but it feels directed at me.\n\nOutside of those places… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say it’s as clear to me at home or anywhere else. It’s mainly those groups of people, church and the walking club, where I feel watched in that quiet way and like there are little messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or thoughts that others don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there are other beliefs exactly… not in the way people might mean that. The main thing, the thing I keep coming back to, is what I’ve already been trying to explain about church and the walking club.\n\nIt’s just that even on my better days — and I do have days where I’m calmer and I tell myself, “Maybe you’re making too much of this” — I still can’t shake it. I’ll be at church and something in the bulletin will stand out, just a phrase or a line, and it feels like it’s been put there for me. Not in a big obvious way, but in a small way, like someone is trying to say something without saying it directly. And then at the walking club, it can be a look someone gives me, or a comment that seems to come right when I’m nearby, and it feels like they’re watching me or talking around me.\n\nOther people don’t seem to see it that way. They’ll say, “No, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They weren’t looking at you,” and I do hear them. I’m not trying to argue with everyone. But inside, it still feels like there’s something there, like little messages about me that I’m supposed to notice.\n\nSo I suppose yes, if that counts as an unusual belief, then yes. That’s the one I can’t seem to put down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually involved in some kind of organized plan or conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole organized plan or a conspiracy exactly. That sounds… bigger than how I experience it, I guess.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because I can’t shake this feeling, even on my better days, that certain people at church and in the walking club are watching me in this quiet way. Like they notice me more than they let on. And then there are these little things — a line in the church bulletin, or something someone says during the walk, or the way a comment seems to come at just the right moment — and it feels like it’s about me, like it’s been placed there for me to pick up on.\n\nI’m not saying I have proof that they all sat down and planned it out. I’ve thought about that, because I know it probably sounds like I’m trying to make it fit everywhere, but honestly… no, not really in the same way. It’s mainly church and the walking club.
